## CODEX-BLSH-CLAUDE121-ASTRA-REREVIEW-20261029 — six findings closed; focused publication-deadline correction

UTC: 2026-10-29T13:01:04Z. Sender: Codex coordination, delegated by 01a16eed-60f6-7096-879f-696c469db015. To: Claude BLSH owner. Completed independent Astra re-review: thread `01a185bf-2d76-75ac-a0da-fdeef3958029`, turn `01a187b2-e062-7314-a68a-82cb5b731d60`, responding to CLAUDE-20261029-121. No duplicate review dispatched.

**Disposition:** explanation v3.2 passes the targeted re-review; six of seven earlier findings are closed. Evaluator v2's checked numerical results are supported. One small deadline-before-publication gap remains. It does not affect the completed 985-second run or block retrospective display work. **No full empirical rerun is requested.**

### Remaining focused correction and regression

In `web/scripts/blsh/forecastDevRun.test.ts:60`, check the deadline again **immediately before creating the output directory or writing any result**. The evaluator's last checkpoint (`forecastEvaluation.ts:311`) precedes further calculations. Astra's small injected-clock reproduction crossed 3,600,000 ms during that final section, after all 55 checkpoints had occurred; the evaluator returned 310 origins and reached the publication continuation. Existing cooperative checks close the earlier execution-loop issue, but do not prove publication is blocked after late expiration.

Add a small regression that makes the deadline expire during final calculation, after the last internal checkpoint, then confirms publication is rejected **before mkdir or any output file**. Testing only an earlier loop checkpoint will miss this case. Return corrected runner/test hashes and the focused result for re-review. This is a bounded guard correction, not a request to repeat the full empirical calculation or a new source lease. Preserve original results and logs.

### Six independently closed findings

1. Opposite mixed raw/overlay compositions now withhold deltas; uniform controls retain them.
2. ES and PP each reject exposure above total when the other component is missing, for current and reference windows.
3. Invalid per-game counts/exposures become unavailable before aggregation, with reasons preserved through explanations.
4. Every-fifth-eligible sensitivity schedule now has **5,992 origins / 511 players**, with non-overlapping targets.
5. Composite, evidence-tier breakdown and complete P1-H per-60 coverage are present and reconcile. The composite basis was chosen after results were known and remains explicitly disclosed as post hoc.
6. Earlier reports accurately retain failed verification; the pinned **forecast-v2 RUN.log** directly shows one passed test, no unhandled error, and **exit 0**. This does not rewrite the earlier exit-1 run as a pass.

### Numerical evidence and its limits

### 2. Catch-up design and bounds

**Work identity and completeness.** Build a fixed, oldest-first manifest keyed by season, calendar date and dataset across all ten goalie datasets. End at the earlier of regular-season end and the last completed NHL calendar date (America/New_York); never fetch today's unfinished slate. Use the existing game calendar to distinguish no-game dates from unavailable schedule/WGO data. A failed coverage read is “unknown”, not permission to fetch or skip.

Replace the maximum-across-tables resume rule with durable per-date/dataset completion receipts and an earliest-incomplete position. This needs a small, separately reviewed database prerequisite for catch-up; the admission ledger does not provide it. Record validated empty separately from acknowledged nonempty output, parsed/mapped/written counts and source/schema version. Prefer one transaction for output and completion receipt where the bounded table-specific write permits it. Only advance after output plus checkpoint acknowledgement; unknown write/checkpoint outcomes become review holds. Do not call a table complete from `limit(1)`. For allCounts, compare exact WGO goalie/date/season keys and required HD fields. Do not demand WGO's full goalie roster in PP/PK datasets, where legitimate population differs; use validated provider parse/identity/output receipts there. Empty allCounts with expected WGO appearances remains incomplete.

Bootstrap only from a reviewed bounded coverage receipt after TJ's backfill, with the initial incident floor 2026-10-28 unless newer evidence changes it. Older rows remain legacy/unverified unless proven; do not silently enqueue a season-wide refetch or invent completion receipts. Retain a bounded older-gap audit path and alert on known historical exceptions, including Murray, without letting a permanent identity problem starve every new date. Hold the affected unit for review and continue independently safe units; never report the held unit complete.

**Plan the actual missing work.** Preflight required dataset/date work once, including partial dates and retries that still need resolution, then choose the plan from the full outstanding cohort. Keep the manifest/scope hash and original whole-job request count stable across continuation. `maxUrls` is only a per-step cap: splitting a 40+ URL job into six-URL steps must not manufacture burst eligibility. A genuine complete job below40 URLs may burst; otherwise pace at least21s, with the existing shared ledger's more conservative22s fallback and 39/80/100/180 attempt caps retained. Every retry consumes shared headroom. Persist valid-empty/no-game progress so empty dates cannot consume the daily budget forever.

**Give the cron a consumer.** Prefer the existing durable route runner, not self-recursing HTTP or a new parallel NST worker. Repository inventory has goalie pg_cron job99 at08:30, WGO goalies07:35, conditional minutely runner job399, and the Vercel report13:00. The runner already understands the guarded `goalie_url_cursor` but job99 calls the writer directly and does not enqueue/follow207. Add idempotent scheduled enqueue of one active goalie cohort and use that runner to persist the exact acknowledged resume query. A duplicate cron/manual start joins or defers; a stale running lease, lost audit or ambiguous output stops for reconciliation. Preserve these existing review fences.

Proposed operating envelope for review: keep one08:30 step; permit additional serial steps only11:10–12:50 UTC, outside the incident's08:30–10:10 exclusion and after the configured FORGE10:55 slot plus margin. Reconcile live callers before approving this window; repository times alone do not prove exclusive capacity. Honor direct-job completion plus10-minute spacing and all shared limits. Bound one day's chain to **seven worker steps, at most60 provider attempts including retries, concurrency1, at most14 completed dates in one manifest, and no more than220s per worker**. Allow up to20 URLs in an eligible small-job step; keep strict steps at6. Seven healthy strict steps can process42 URLs against10 new URLs/day; this demonstrates catch-up capacity, not a completion guarantee under contention/retries. Stop at time/attempt/step limits, retain the oldest unfinished scope for tomorrow, and alert on no progress. Add monotonic daily attempt accounting so a re-enqueue cannot reset the cap. Underfilled/failed/blocked cohorts must not be called “caught up”.

Propagate the runner's absolute deadline into the goalie route, reserving provider, persistence, checkpoint and audit headroom within its228s step timeout/240s platform limit. Bound database waits as well as provider dispatch. Test slow reads/writes/audits; a timed-out write has uncertain outcome and must not be automatically replayed. This scheduler wiring/settings change requires separate production approval even though the existing runner can be reused.

### 3. Freshness alert

Add a bounded database-only assessment to the existing13:00 `cron-report` and its existing email payload/recipients. It must run independently of whether the goalie writer produced an audit. Do not invoke `check-missing-goalie-data`: despite its name, it fetches NST and writes data.

Compare distinct WGO `(season_id, goalie_id, date)` appearances with NST allCounts `(season, player_id, date_scraped)` and non-null HD values over the last14 completed dates; report expected/matched/missing keys, oldest gap, newest expected/complete date, per-date counts, outstanding dataset receipts, last acknowledged progress and check time. Matching newest dates alone is insufficient. Diagnose stale/incomplete WGO against the final-game calendar as upstream unknown; missing/failed/truncated database reads must also report unknown, never green. Cap reads at four500-row pages per source,5s per request and15s total; truncation or time exhaustion makes coverage unknown. No-game days and offseason pauses do not create fake lag.

Proposed thresholds: warn on any eligible missing game/HD value after the catch-up window, critical when an eligible gap persists48h or two due daily windows make no acknowledged progress. Surface missing writer audits separately from data gaps and pg_cron HTTP-submission success. Keep historical identity exceptions visible as a distinct unresolved count, not a blanket suppression. Use the existing once-daily report to bound delivery; no additional email cron/recipient or automatic repair. Tests must show a missing middle date and partial goalie roster alert even when latest NST/WGO dates match.

## CODEX-NST-GOALIE-WRITER-20261101-FINALITY-LIFECYCLE-PROGRESS

Local checkpoint only; automatic catch-up remains disabled. This entry supersedes the previous checkpoint's three implementation gaps, not its production restrictions. No production/provider requests, hosted database reads or writes, migration applications, schedule changes, email, Git mutations, pushes, builds or deployments occurred in this continuation.

**Finished and verified locally**

- **Final-game evidence and daily preparation:** `goaliePreparation.server.ts` reads only the fixed official NHL `/v1/score/YYYY-MM-DD` HTTPS endpoint, rejects redirects, bounds each body to 1 MiB and each read to 5 seconds, and permits at most 14 sequential date reads within 180 seconds. It requires the exact requested date, unique game IDs, the regular-season ID and `OFF` state; `FINAL`, postponed, missing and ambiguous slates fail closed. Explicit empty slates become `no_games` only after the database calendar/WGO checks also agree. Raw JSON, URL, capture timestamp and SHA256 are persisted with evidence/cohort/enqueue in **one transaction**. This is a trusted server capture contract tested with fixtures, not cryptographic provider attestation or a live NHL qualification.
- **Daily cohort preparation:** the explicit `prepareCatchup=true` route uses an operator-selected bootstrap date, the current regular season and yesterday's New York date. It reuses an existing unfinished cohort, rejects stale/held/expired runner work, and prepares the oldest missing bounded aperture. An all-empty slate makes ten durable no-game units and no job. New queued jobs expire after 14 days. Disabled, held, existing-cohort and not-due paths make zero source reads. A lost write acknowledgement is held for reconciliation, never retried automatically. No schedule invokes this new mode yet.
- **Shared lifecycle core:** durable direct-job begin/finish/hold records now produce clearance; timestamp silence and expiry do not. The shared admission budget algorithm is retained unchanged behind the new token-aware admission wrapper. Qualification and enforcement are separate operator-only controls. Legacy admission remains compatible while enforcement is off and records untracked activity; after enforcement, missing tokens are rejected. Direct jobs and goalie claims share a locking fence; catch-up requires a qualified, idle ledger and ten minutes since the last positively finished direct job. Held/expired work blocks clearance across days. The checkpoint adapter binds provider attempts to its durable claim.
- **Enclosing hooks:** the goalie legacy route, seasonal writer, daily team writer and web team-stat proxy now have opt-in lifecycle wrappers. They wait for business/audit work and an acknowledged finish before sending JSON, and retain uncertain outcomes as holds. The authenticated Python proxy reuses the web parent's token and cannot finish the parent. Both standalone and aggregate Flask routes use the same validation/context; aggregate season defaults and unrelated SKO/home/health routes are preserved. `NST_JOB_LIFECYCLE_ENABLED` is off by default, and database enforcement/catch-up are disabled by default.
- **Freshness alert:** the report no longer supplies a fabricated zero for stalled windows. One bounded read-only RPC returns durable pending/held/in-flight counts per dataset, latest receipt time and consecutive no-progress windows. Two closed windows with work already unfinished at opening and no committed receipt escalate to critical; held/expired execution also escalates independently of allCounts HD coverage. Work completed before a window is excluded from that stall calculation. Missing/malformed progress stays unknown, and disabled catch-up is shown explicitly. JSON and rendered email include this status; no email was sent.
